1  Introduction

     1.1    Program Introduction

        The initial exploration/delineation drilling in Azadegan oil field has proved the presence
       of a very prolific hydrocarbon bearing sedimentary layers, particularly within the Cretaceous
       sequences. Sarvak formation being the main reservoir is divided to 13 zones (Sarvak-1 to
      Sarvak-12 and Sarvak Intra). The Sarvak-1 to Sarvak-10 zones are oil bearing with a tilted
     WOC.

        The well S372 a highly deviated well, is planned to be drilled in the well Pad-47 as a
      Sarvak producer well. This pad is located in the middle part of South Azadegan oil field in
       Area-I. Figure 1 shows the position of S372 well in this Pad.

        The objective of this well is producing from Sarvak-3 and 4 to provide maximum drainage
      from reservoir. According to the Petro-physical log of AZN-10 which is the closest well to
       pad-47, Sarvak-3 and Sarvak-4 are capable for production.
         Although Sarvak 6 and 7 have appropriate reservoir properties but these zones are near to
       water transition zone. Well S372 has been designed with azimuth of 125 degree according to
      keep the proper distance from AZN-10 and AZNS-36 wells.

                                       Figure 1

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084 (S372)                                               Page 10

     1.2    South Azadegan Structure

      The Azadegan  field  is located on a large long-axis  anticline with N-S trending, an
 approximate length of 60 km and width of 20 km. The South Azadegan  oil  field  is the
 southern  part  of  the Azadegan oil field. The trap area of the south Azadegan is limited to
 43.2x17.2 km. The Azadegan Oil field was  discovered  in  1999, when Well AZN-1 was
 drilled  vertically down  to the Cretaceous Fahliyan formation with total depth of 4,643m. The
 results of this well, revealed the reservoir characteristics of the field. Following to the result of the
 well no.AZN-1, another 5 exploration wells was drilled in different parts of this huge field down to
 Fahliyan Formation. In the later stage the well No.10 assumed as exploration well for evaluation
 of deeper formations. This well was drilled in 2008, down to the depth of 5315 m, mainly to
 evaluate the Jurassic formations (Figure 2).

       This new well that located in the middle part of South Azadegan oil field in Area-I will be
 drilled directionally to the reservoirs in the Sarvak Formations, and will be completed by
 perforated liner. Production from the Sarvak is considered for producing Azadegan field in order
 to evaluate potential producing of South Azadegan oil field.

                                       Figure 2

   5 Reservoir Data

   6 Key Reservoir Description

      Sarvak Formation

 The Sarvak Formation is regionally overlain by Laffan shale with unconformity and Comprises

 thick limestone and dolomitic limestone with minor clayey and argillaceous interlays, attaining

 totally 600~700m in thickness. For the importance of hydrocarbon accumulation, which is

 lithologically dominated by bioclastic wackestone and packstone of shallow marine origin, whereas

 the lower part mainly comprises argillaceous wackestone and mudstone, representing a more basin

 setting. The upper part of the formation is to be defined arbitrarily as the interval from the top of the

 formation down to the seismic marker, Intra-Sarvak.

      Sar-Intra   204    257.4    237.7    145      61        0       15.8     16.1      8.3

 For the layering scheme of the reservoir section, sequence stratigraphic concept was applied to the

 Azadegan wells. Judging from the characteristics of gamma ray and other wireline logs, relative sea-

 level change during the deposition of the reservoir sections were interpreted in terms of sequence

 stratigraphic context. Based upon the sea-level change inferred, the sections were interpreted as

 vertical accumulation of a number of zones, each of which is geologically termed as a depositional

 sequence, implicitly representing a genetically related body of sedimentary sequence. The formation

 was divided totally into thirteen zones by interpreting

   Sar-1
 Gross thickness of Sar-1 is in the range of 16m to 26m and the average gross is 20.9m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 71.1% and Net-pay is 11.4m. Although properties (Porosity is 12.5%, Perm is

 10.6md) are higher, oiliness (So is 57.7%) is lower, It comprises mainly light brown, brown and

 bituminous oiliness limestone with fossils, rudist debris and echinoid chalky, Nezzazata.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 15

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

        NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

    Sar-2
 Gross thickness of Sar-2 is in the range of 8m to 19m and the average gross is 14.6m in thickness

 with  the lower Net/Gross  ratio  of 13.7%.  It  is mainly composed  of  bioclastic  mudstone,

 lamellational limestone and argillaceous limestone. Terrigenous mud is present. Property is poor.

 Mean porosity is 9.3% and mean permeability is 1.8md.This zone is a compact layer and can be a

 stable barrier.
    Sar-3
 Gross thickness of Sar-3 is in the range of 16m to 30m with average of 21.4m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 79% and Net-pay is 15.8m. It is mainly composed of light brown or dark brown

 bituminous oil dolomitic limestone, which is dominated by bioturbated and bioclastic packstones

 and grainstone. It is a main oil-bearing zone with the best property and oiliness of Sarvak formation

 in the Azadegan. Mean porosity is 16.8% and mean permeability is 20.7md and So is 83%.
    Sar-4
 Gross thickness of Sar-4 ranges from 19m to 33m and the average gross is 24.5m in thickness and

 Net/Gross ratio is 77.2% and Net-pay is 17.6m. Light brown, brown and dark brown limestone with

 fossils or bituminous dominate and majority is peloidal or bioclastic wackestone, packstone and

 grainstone containing slight mudstone. Property and oiliness are moderate. Mean porosity is 12.7%

 and mean permeability is 8.5md and So is 69%.
    Sar-5
 Gross thickness of Sar-5 is in range of 21m to 37m and the average gross is 28.2m in thickness. The

 higher Net/Gross ratio is 91% and the thicker Net-pay is 24.6m. It comprises brown green gray

 calcareous shale, brown to darkbrown oil bituminous limestone and bioclastic limestone with fossils,

 Nezzazata, rudist debris, Ovalveolina, Dukhania, Cisalveolis and Miliolid. Majority is wackestone

 and packstone. Property and oiliness are moderate next to Sarvak-3 and Sarvak-8. Mean porosity is

 12.7% and mean permeability is 8.2md and So is 70%.
    Sar-6
 Gross thickness of Sar-6 is in range of 29m to 54m and the average gross is 41.4m in thickness with

 the higher Net/Gross ratio of 86.1% and the thicker Net-pay of 26.2m. It is lithologiclly dominated

 by light brown, brown and dark brown bituminous limestone with fossils, Nezzazata, Rudistdebris,

 Ovalveolina, Dukhania, Cisalveolis, Miliolid, Echinoid and partly with oil. Majority is peloidal and

 bioclastic wackestone, packstone and grainstone. Both property and oiliness are moderate. Mean

 porosity is 12.1% and mean permeability is 4.3md and So is 69.6%.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 16

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

        NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

      Sar-7
 Gross thickness of Sar-7 is in ranges of 11m to 20m and the average gross is 15.6m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 78.5% and Net-pay is 6m. It is lithologically dominated by light brown to dark

 brown bituminous limestone with foraminifera and comprises bioclastic packstone, wackestone and

 mudstone. Property and oiliness are poor. Mean porosity is 10% and mean permeability is 1.4md

 and So is 63.8%.
    Sar-8
 Gross thickness of Sar-8 is in range of 47m to 72m and the average gross is 57.3m in thickness with

 the higher Net/Gross ratio of 98% and the thicker Net-pay of 14.1m. It is mainly composed of thick

 light brown to darkbrown bituminous limestone with fossils, Nezzazata, Rudistdebris, Ovalveolina,

 Dukhania, Cisalveolis, Miliolid, Echinoid with middle-thin buff limestone interbed and partly with

  oil. Bioclastic packstone and grainstone dominate. Property and oiliness are better next to Sarvak-3

 of Sarvak formation in the Azadegan. Mean porosity is 15.3% and mean permeability is 13.5md

 and So is 75%, but It is an oil and water bearing zone.
    Sar-9
 Gross thickness of Sar-9 is in range of 16m to 31m and the average gross is 23.3m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 77.4% and Net-pay is only 3.2m. Lithology is dominated by light brown or brown

 limestone with fossils and bituminous. It is an oil and water-bearing zone. Property is moderate.

 Mean porosity is 12.3% and mean permeability is 3.7md. Oiliness is poor and So is 61.3%.
    Sar-10
 Gross thickness of Sar-10 is in range of 20m to 37m and the average gross is 31.2m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 60.8% and Net-pay is only 1.5m. Property and oiliness is very poor. Mean

 porosity is 10.8% and mean permeability is 8.3md and So is 53.6%. It comprises light brown to

 brown and buff limestone and partly with oil and marl interbeds. It is an oil and water-bearing zone.
    Sar-11
 Gross thickness of Sar-11 is in range of 58m to 96m and the average gross is 80.8m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 63.2% and it is a water-bearing zone. Lithology is dominated by Light brown,

 brown and buff limestone with slight dolomite. Property is moderate. Mean porosity is 13.4% and

 mean permeability is 6.3md. It is a water-bearing zone.
    Sar-12
 Gross thickness of Sar-12 is in range of 36m to 62m and the average gross is 48.4m in thickness.

 Net/Gross ratio is 87.6% and it is a water-bearing and no oil pay zone. Lithology of this zone is

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 17

 mainly Light brown, brown and buff limestone with slight dolomite and argillaceous limestone.

 Property is better. Mean porosity is 17.6% and mean permeability is 21.4md. It is a water-bearing

 zone.
    Sar_Intra
 Gross thickness of Sar_intra is in range of 204m to 257m and the average gross is 237.7m in

 thickness. Net/Gross ratio is 61% and it is a water bearing and no oil pay zone. It is dominated by

 thick limestone with thin shale interbeds and involves light gray to light brown dolomitic and chalky

 limestone with some cherts, light brown coarse limestone containing pyrite and oil crack, light

 brown or gray medium-coarse grains limestone, brown and light gray argillaceous limestone, green

 gray to gray sticky marl and black medium hard slight calcareous shale interbeds. Property is better.

 Mean porosity is 15.8% and mean permeability is 16.1md. It is a water-bearing zone too.

   11    Rig Move

             Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:
          o     Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.
          o     Safe standby areas for rig in case move have to be suspended.
          o     Loss of trucks in case of repair.
          o     Moving at night.
             Hold  pre-move  safety  meeting.  Ensure  everyone  understands  individual
              Responsibilities.
            Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.
            Commence rig move to AZNS#084, S372, PAD NO. 47
              Position rig for optimal operations
             Rig up on cellar
              Finish rig up and commence operational procedure for AZNS#084.
              Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.
              Test all manifold and surface safety valves
          o   RKB – GLE and Elevation
          o     Spud date and time.
            Mud tanks should be empty during move. Fill water tanks and keep as full as
              possible.

   12    Pre Spud

1)  Certificate
              All rig equipment are on location, properly rigged up and function-tested. Check
        availability and content of hoisting equipment inspection & maintenance records according to
      API.
2)  Safety Device
              Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report;
         test derrick man escape device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on daily
        drilling report; test rig intercom system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-Mud
      Logger communication link.
             Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g. fire extinguishers, eye-wash stations etc.,
         is installed at the proper locations as per rig layout diagrams.
              Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support drilling
       unit during whole well period and that mast is centred above rotary table.
             Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig
        floor hoisting equipment are adequate for handling 8-1/2″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure
        that mechanical tong torque gauges for drill pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have
      been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.
3) Rig Equipment
              Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed, and
      26″ bit breakers are on the rig floor. Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools available
        for vertical section survey.
            Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been
        function-tested with charging pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate
       screen inventory for the shale shakers is available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure
       water tanks are completely filled and transfer lines from water tanks to mud system are hooked
       up. Make sure all mud materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section are on location, in
      sound condition and suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and sufficient
      volume has been built for top section and for well-kill.
            Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts:
         liners, pistons, valves, seats etc. Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with Nitrogen
        to the proper pre-charge pressure, 30% of expected standpipe pressure during drilling of the 26″
       hole section; check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available on location to adjust pulsation
      dampener pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off valves are set at
       the proper pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge lines from the pop-off
       valves to the mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all high-pressure hoses are properly
       secured with safety chains. P/T should be done to high-pressure surface line of circulating
       system.

4) Wellhead & BOP
             Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m
      above cellar bottom to drain cement from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 25

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                        NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                               Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                            South Azadegan Oil field

        NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                              PEDEC

        casing. Verify that pre-installed 30″ conductor pipe is vertical and that no angle/bend exists
      between conductor and 30″ riser pipe.
            Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and ready
        to be run, float shoe should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on the pipe-
       rack prior to spud in the well.
             Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested
       properly.
5) Others
              Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig
      consumption available on site.
            Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is ok.
             Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location.
       Discuss procedures involved throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and critical
        safety operation measures.

       NIDC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed  it in  rigs. The checklist will be

          performed based on the aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following

           matters should be checked:

            Check & observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in

                         four stages; rig levelling, derrick verticality, derrick alignment with rotary

                            table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe. To do this pick up 3 joints

                         of 9 1/2” DC and check the verticality of the derrick.

             Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as :

                      Desander, Desilter, mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers, mud

                     guns & mixers, should be all available on rig and functioning properly.

             All mud tanks, mud transfer lines & connection should be checked by filling

                     them with water & cross wellhead circulation.

            Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing

                       equipment. The calibration should be done under supervision of well site

                            drilling supervisor before starting the operation.

            Weight indicator, rotary speed gauge, any smart drilling instrumentation, Top

                         Drive, Stroke meter, pressure gauges on manifold and in the drilling console,

                           loss / gain gauge and any other gauges should be checked and calibrated.

             Stand pipe manifold valves & connection must be checked & pressure tested.

             All motors should be checked under load prior to start drilling.

     1 STRUCTURAL GEOLOGY:

      The Azadegan oil field is located at 80km west of Ahwaz and the Azadegan Field is an
 elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belongs to Arabian Trend which is different
 from “Zagros Trend”. There is no outcrop for Azadegan structure and it was detected by 3D
 seismic interpretation.

     2 LITHO-STRATIGRAPHY DESCRIPTION:

      The stratigraphy sequence of the field includes Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh,
 Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan Formations, from
 top to bottom. This sequence in well no. AZN-10, the deepest up to now drilled well of the
 field, continues with Garau, Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and Neyriz formations.
   Fars Group
    Fars group originally divided into two formations, Aghajari and Gachsaran formations.
   Aghajari Formation
     Depth: Surface to 1147    D r i l l e d Thickness: 1147m    Age: MIOCENE-
 PLIOCENE Lithology: This Formation begins with light cream, light gray, yellow, reddish
 brown, quartz, subrounded, good  sorted Sandstone. This formation  is mostly
 consisting  of  red marl interbedded with sandstone and red claystone layers. The Red
 claystone increase toward the base of the formation. The Sandstones are variably
 consolidated and generally light brown, light gray in colour, fine to medium grain size,
 generally fine grained, rarely coarse grained, generally well sorted and dominated by
 sub-angular to sub-rounded quartz grains, loose, friable to moderately hard, slightly
 calcareous. The Marls are red brown to brown and pale red brown, generally sticky and
 slightly silty, soft to firm and locally very low calcareous grading to claystone. The Red
 claystones are brown, moderate to dark brown, generally soft, sticky and amorphous,
 locally some calcareous grading to marl, generally non silty and occasionally mottled. A
 fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.
   Gachsaran Formation
     Depth: 1147-1502 m   Drilled Thickness: 355 m      Age: Middle Miocene - Early
 Miocene Lithology: This Formation begins with white, glassy, soft, medium, hard
 Anhydrite and  off white, light gray to gray, soft to medium hard, Dolomitic Limestone.
 Comprises of anhydrite interbedded with gray marl and red marl, salt beds of variable
 thickness and some thin limestone beds. This formation in type section not divided to other
 member. The Anhydrite is white off white, milky, soft, partly crystalline to crystalline,
 medium hard to hard. The Gray marl  is gray to light gray, soft. The Red marl is red
 brown to brown, soft, sticky. The Limestone is gray to light gray, buff to buff gray, hard,
 slightly dolomitic.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 29

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                        NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                               Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                            South Azadegan Oil field

        NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                              PEDEC

  Asmari Formation
       Depth: 1502-1785m        Drilled Thickness: 283 m          Age: Oligocene -
   Miocene Lithology: This Formation begins with cream, light gray, soft to medium hard,
   poor Fossil Dolomitic Limestone. Consisting of white, off white, light gray to gray, soft
    to medium hard, I/II,I, II, Dolomitic Limestone particularly in upper part. The
   Sandstone increase toward the base with interbedded gray marl, dolomite and claystone of
    the beds formation. The Sandstone is gray, light to gray, medium to coarse with partly
    pyritic, medium sorted. The Gray marl is gray, light gray, soft. The Dolomite is light
   cream to cream, porous. The Claystone is red light brown, soft, sticky.

  Pabdeh Formation
        Depth: 1785-2193m        Drilled Thickness: 408m          Age: Paleocene-Eocene
   The Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field.

   Lithology:  This  formation  starts  with  Marl,  gradually  replaces  by  Argillaceous
   Limestone. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown Argillaceous Limestone and
   Marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part is
   composed  of  dark-brown,  slightly  Argillaceous  Limestone.  Pelagic  fauna,  mostly
   Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to
    late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene deposits exist in the Azadegan area.

  Gurpi Formation
   This is formation consists of three main units:
   A) Upper Gurpi Member
   B) Tarbour Tongue
   C) Lower Gurpi Member

  Upper Gurpi Member
     Depth: 2193-2314 m     Drilled Thickness: 121m    Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian

   Lithology: This Member begins with light gray, gray, off white, soft to medium , sticky
   Argillaceous limestone. The Upper Gurpi Member is mostly consisting of gray buff to gray
   with of trace partly buff gray, dark gray, medium hard to hard argillaceous, high
   argillaceous graded to marl Argillaceous limestone interbedded with anhydrite marl and
   chalky limestone beds. The Anhydritic marl is light gray to gray, soft, sticky, graded to
   high argillaceous calcareous. The Chalky Limestone is light gray to buff gray, medium
   hard to soft,chalky II,A slightly argillaceous.
   Tarbour Tongue
     Depth: 2314-2410m      Drilled Thickness: 96m      Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian
 Lithology: This Member begins with cream, light brown, porosity, oil stained Limestone.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 30

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                        NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                               Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                            South Azadegan Oil field

        NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                              PEDEC

 Tarbour Tongue consisting of cream to light gray, light brown, buff, medium hard, II,A
 Limestone interbedded with chalky limestone and marl beds. The Chalky limestone is light
 cream, soft. The Dolomite medium bedded dark gray. The Marl is light gray to gray, soft,
 sticky.

   Lower Gurpi Member
     Depth:2410-2542m     Drilled  Thickness: 132m     Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian
 Lithology: This Member begins with off white, cream, soft to medium hard Argillaceous
 limestone and light gray to gray, soft, sticky Marl. The Member consisting of dark gray
 to gray, light gray, soft to medium hard Argillaceous limestone and light cream and soft. II,
 A. Chalky Limestone beds.

   Bangestan Group
    Bangestan group is divided into four formations: Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak and Kazhdumi.

   Ilam Formation
     Depth: 2542-2699m       Drilled Thickness: 157m     Age: Coniacian -Santonian
 Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of limestone interbedded with shale and
 clay stone beds. The Limestone is cream, buff to buff gray, light brown, soft to medium
 hard, chalky, II, A, Oil stained Partly slightly argillaceous. The Shale is green gray to light
 green gray, mostly brown gray, medium hard, pyritic, slightly calcareous. The Clay stone is
 green gray to light green gray and soft, slightly calcareous.

   Laffan Formation
    Depth: 2699-2712m           Drilled Thickness: 13m           Age: Coniacian

 Lithology: This Formation begins with greenish gray, green, Soft, sticky, pyrite, slightly and
 calcareous Claystone and green, light greenish gray, light gray, medium to hard, fissile
 Shale. This formation is mostly consisting of shale interbedded with clay stone beds. The
 Shale green gray to light green gray, mostly brown gray medium hard, pyritic, medium hard,
 slightly calcareous. The Clay stone green gray to light green gray, soft and slightly
 calcareous.

   Sarvak Formation
    Depth: 2712-3600m             Drilled Thickness: 888m        Age: Cenomanian

 Lithology: This Formation begins with light brown, cream, light cream, I/II, I and Oil stained
 Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of off white, cream, buff to cream and white
 to cream, soft, soft to medium hard, chalky Limestone interbedded with claystone,
 argillaceous limestone, shale, argillaceous dolomite and dolomite limestone beds. The
 Claystone is gray, soft, sticky, high calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is white gray to
 light gray, light buff, medium hard and slightly bituminous. The Shale is dark brown, dark
 gray, black, medium hard and calcareous. The Argillaceous Dolomite is buff gray, hard. The

 Dolomitic Limestone is buff gray to light brown gray, hard, densely. The Dolomitic
 Limestone is gray, partly light gray, medium hard.

  2 Lesson learned

   Understanding and anticipating  drilling problems, understanding  their causes, and planning

    solutions are necessary for overall-well-cost control and for successfully reaching the target zone.

   According to report of nearby wells, the following items has been considered to drilling the new

    well:

      1-  Bit balling is common problem. A rough bit surface will increase the surface area and

         increase the adhesive forces. When the mud flow stops, or if a bit with poor hydraulic

         design is used, an electrostatic force may cause clays to stick in the bit surface. With several

        work such as increase flow rate, Pump a caustic pill, Spin the bit as fast as possible can be

         prevented this problem.

      2- Stuck  is another problems  in the perevius  wells. stuck can be  classified under two

          categories: differential and mechanical. In the nearby wells, it can be said that Mechanical

         type wasn’t happened except stuck 13 3/8” casing in AZN 047 at 765m (Aghajari formation

        and got free with 250 Klbs overpull). More stuck in the mentioned wells often is associated

         with complete loss, so differential type may be happened. In AZN 047, string got stuck with

        71 PCF mud from 1928m to 1945m (Pabdeh formation) and got free with 40 Klbs Over

           pull. Another stuck in this well was happened with 76 PCF mud at 2675m to 2714m

         (Sarvak Formation) and got free with 60-100 Klbs over pull.

      3-  Tight hole is a common problem in the mentioned wells and usally for curing this problem,

       mud weight is increased. The serius tight hole was reported in Jahrum/Pabdeh and Gurpi. It

         should be noted that when tight hole is cured, mud weight don’t excess from critical value.

      4- The costly problem that may be happened in future wells is twist off string. Twisting off is

         ocurred when the drill string parts by failing catastrophically under the torsional stress. This

       may happen if the drill string has been encountered either fatigue or excessive torque. This

        problem has been occurred four times in AZN 025(2660m, 3445m, 3472m and 3542m) and

          fish was recovered. Because of string got stuck, twist off  bit sub was happened in

        Jahrum/Pabdeh formation. Due to fish hasn’t been recovered, side tracking was perform

        from 1533m.

    WELL AZN 17H:

      5- The 17 ½” hole section had been drilled to 1140m with 70-82 PCF W.B.M, the formation

          drilled  is Aghajari-Gachsaran,  tight hole (1140-50m) was encounter when during the

         condition trip, wash and ream several times this interval. Totally operation is smoothly, a

         proper mud weight in this section is important for the well’s operations.

      6- While drilled 17 ½” and 12 ¼” hole section in Aghajari and Gachsaran formation observed

           bit and stabilizer balled up.

      7- The best approach is to use good fundamentals: an inhibitive mud, optimal hydraulics and a

          quality ROP enhancer.

                                      -   Cure bit balling

                                      -  Work the bit by rotating fast off bottom and reciprocating the string to

                          shake off the clay coating.

                                      -  Reduce ROP for a short time.

                                      -   Spot a walnut pill.

                                      -   Circulation.

                                      -   Fresh water.

                                      -    If bit balling is observed while drilling, a detergent is usually added to

                             disperse the balled clays.

                                      -  The use of a small percentage of glycol also helps to reduce bit balling.

                                      -   Consider fresh water pill.

                                      -  Keep the API fluid loss as low as possible.

      8- The 8 ½” hole is drilled to 1970m with 75 pcf salt water w/2-12 BPH losses. For the mud

          losses, a mud system with LCM is required.

      9- The 6 ⅛” hole was drilled to 3595.5m MD with 76 PCF O.B.M, the formation drilled was

        Gachsaran-Asmari-Pabdeh-Ilam-Sarvak-Kazhdumi, the main problem  is stuck pipe @

        3595.5m , MD in Kazhdumi Fm. work on stuck with over pull, pipe got free and continue

          drilled to TD with 73 PCF O.B.M so a proper mud weight in this section is important for

          the well’s operations.

     10- In well AZNS-026, mud properties in 17 ½” interval are poor due to the shortage of

          materials such as polymer, ultrafine powder and LCM. This led to time lost about 25days. It

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 41

            is recommended that KCL-polymer mud system be adopted.

     11- In 8 ½” interval of well AZNS-027, overflow occurred at 2663m in Ilam formation, then

           lost  circulation happened  at  interval 2342-2381m when mud  weight  increased  for

          controlling  kicking, 25 days  lost  in  treating  loss  of  circulation and  kicking.  It  is

        recommended that the pump  rate  is no more than 500gpm in formations which are

         vulnerable to lost circulation such as Asmari and Gurpi. Meanwhile, mud weight shall not

        be changed sharply.

     12- In 8 ½ interval of well AZNS-027, lost circulation control operation was executed 6 times

         with kinds of LCM such as coarse, mediate and fine nutshell, mica and so on, but it was not

          controlled entirely. Maybe cementing plug is the most efficient measurement to control lost

          circulation.

     13- In 8 ½” hole section of well AZNS 045 while coring operation due to pump hi- vis. Pill

         before coring, plug string and pipe sticking. Back off string from Slick sub, fishing

         operation was not successful and finally side track hole.  It  is recommended that NO

      PUMPING PILL BERFORE CORING. before back off String, observe shoot up pressure

         while coring operation, It is recommended that if observe pressure shoot up while coring

         stop operation and pull out string for checking.

      Well AZN 12 in 12 ¼” hole section:

      Drilling formation was continued to 1441m, due to tight hole, MW was increased to 77 pcf.

     Continued  drilling to 1585m, complete  loss was happened. Drilling has been continued

     to1600m meanwhile LCM pill was pumped. Also, MW has been deceased to 75pcf. drilling

     was continued as blind to 1860m W/ 250-480 BPH loss and suddenly (pressure drop 500 psi),

      twist off has been occurred F/ 8-1/2" DC. Fish was recovered with Overshoot. Cement was

    pumped (with 40-50 BPH loss) in two stage with 68 pcf mud.

        It is recommended that at first care about MW and hold MW as low as possible and second if

     complete loss is happened, attempt to cure loss by LCM, by pass system and reinforce loss zone

      then continued drilling.

      In this well twist off string was happened several times so be careful about flowing pressure

     while drilling and if observed any pressure drop, it must be check surface equipment and check

      string by slow rate pump. If observed unmoral condition pull string of hole for checking.

     While spud rig, be sure that all down hole equipment has inspeced. Check inspection sheets and

1  26” Hole Section / 20” Surface Casing

      1.1 General

       Casing Point            20” Casing @ 150m (20”, 94#, K-55, BTC)
       Formation                Aghajari
        Risks & Hole problems   No Significant Hole Problem
        Deviation control        Totco
       Logging                       ---
                                  Bentonite Mud
                MW    65-68 pcf
        Drilling Fluid
                                     Vis.    70-80 sec
                        PH    ≥ 11
                   WOB  Maximum Available
        Drilling Parameters    GPM   600-1100
                     RPM   90-130

      1.2 Formation Sampling
             On each 5 meters one sample would be taken. In addition to this general rule,
                   geologist may decide to take more samples.

      1.3 Sequence of Operation

             Make up 26” BHA as per BHA design
             Tag the bottom of cellar and control ROP to start drilling. Drill the first and second
               stands at 60-70 RPM with 300 GPM of low flow rate to avoid washing out the
               conductor.
             Pump Hi-Vis pill to sweep hole when every stand drilled down. Continue drilling to
             ±150 m (20”casing point in Aghajari formation). Pay enough attention to  drill
                  straight hole. Use LCM pill if encountered heavy losses. If loss couldn’t be cured by
         LCM pill next program will be issue later.

           Note:
                - TD should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole under casing shoe.
                - The exact TD will be determined based on 20”casing tally.
                - Do not forget to strap pipe while POOH.
                - Run Totco each 50 m. to evaluate the verticality of hole.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 45

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

                - Use following parameters to drill this hole section:
            -WOB = Maximum available {Pay enough attention to drill straight hole}
             -RPM = 90-130
             -GPM = 600-1100

             At TD, Circulate hole clean. POOH to surface. Check  bit  if balling. Perform
                condition trip. Spot Hi-Vis pills after circulate hole clean. Drop Totco, POOH and
                  retrieve survey tool.
             Hold pre-job safety meeting with involved personnel for running 20″ casing.

             R/U 20” casing running gear & function test 20″ casing running equipment. Perform
                Safety meeting.

             Match the stab-in stinger with float shoe on surface.

             P/U and M/U shoe joint.

             Thread-lock shoe connections. Function test float shoe, Fill up casing with weighted
           mud every joint to keep weight balance between inside casing and annular. Monitor
        WOH during running casing to detect excessive drag.

             Run ±150m (Approx. 13 Jts) 20", 94 ppf, K-55, BTC casing with stab-in type float
               shoe to bottom as per casing program (install centralizers as per casing tally). Casing
               shoe should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough stick
              up (1.5 m) on rig floor. Fill casing by non LCM fresh mud joint by joint.

             Rig up C-plate. Run stab-in stinger (with new O-ring seal), 5″ pup joint (with 5″ x
              20″ bow spring centralizer) and 5″ HWDP as stinger string in 20″ casing
             Slowly lower stinger string and make sure stab into float shoe ok.
             Make up circulating head. Fill up annulus (5″ x 20″) with weighted mud and check
                 for sealing.
             Secure the inner string to the rig substructure with slings.
             Break circulation slowly. And circulate 200% of annular volume with mud at 800-
              1000 GPM.

             Note:
                - Take care when running casing not to set down excessive weight if tight hole or ledges
               are encountered.

             Hook up cement line and pressure test same (Test Pressure:3000 psi).
             Hold pre job safety meeting with related people.
             Cement 20″ casing as per cement program.
             P/U stinger, circulate to flush string inside. POOH.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 46

────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

             WOC.
           Note:
                - Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top-up
              jobs.

              If there is no cement return to surface, a top-up job should be performed to fill the
                annulus, stage by stage until cement reaches the surface. Use the same weight as the
               primary cement slurry. Ensure adequate X-Over for connecting line pipe to cement
                  line is available. Wait for the top job cement to get hard.

             Correctly determine elevation from top flange of CHH to RKB and then Rough cut
              20” landing joint and L/D same. L/D and remove 30” conductor pipe.

             Dress casing neck suitable to install and to weld casing head spool. (If C.H.H is slip
                type at bottom, install it as per Wellhead Engineer Installation).

              Install and weld casing head housing 20 3/4” – 3000 psi on casing stub and test the
                 sealing of welded casing (limit pressure to a maximum of 80% of the collapse
                 strength of the casing or maximum 400 psi). Outlets to align with cellar stairs.

             N/U spacer, kill/choke manifold and 20-3/4″ 3K Hydrill.

             P/T wellhead & BOP system as per NIOC Policy.

              Install wear bushing.

           Note:
                 - Do not forget to check the wear bushing with CHH and tie down flange before N/U
            BOP’s.
             Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:
                         20” Casing Tally
                         20” Casing Report
                         20” Cement Report
                         26” Hole Section Summary
               WHD Test report &  it’s diagram including dimensions and  serial
                          numbers.

2  17-1/2” Hole Section / 13-3/8” Intermediate Casing

      2.1 General

       Casing Point             13-3/8” Casing @ 1202m(68#, N-80, BTC)
       Formation                Aghajari,55 m below top of Gachsaran
        Risks & Hole problems   Loss & tight hole
        Deviation control         Single shot each 100 m & Multi shot each 300 m.
       Logging           GR / Deviation Tool
                                  Bentonite Mud (F/150 TO 300 m.)
                MW     65-68 pcf
        Drilling Fluid(1)
                                   Vis.     70-80 Sec.
                       PH     ≥ 11
                             KCL/Polymer Mud (F/300 TO 1202m.)
                MW     65-81 pcf
        Drilling Fluid(2)
                                   Vis.     40- 50 Sec.
                       PH      9.5 - 10
                   WOB   10 - 50 klb
        Drilling Parameters     RPM   90-150
                     GPM   900-1100

      2.2 Formation Sampling
           From 150m to section TD: On each 2-5 meters one sample would be taken.
             Note: As per well site geologist request, the sample program may be changed.

      2.3 Sequence of Operations

             M/U and RIH with 17-1/2″ drilling BHA.
             Tag TOC. Circulate. Drill out cement and shoe track with reduced parameters.
              Drill with reduced parameters till stabilizer has passed through casing shoe.
             Continue drilling to 300 m, Displace new mud into hole.
              Drill ahead with optimized parameters.
             Take survey following (Single shot each 100 m. & Multi shot each 300 m.).
             Short trip every 300m or depending on hole condition.
              Drill hole to section TD, perform wiper trip, circulate, spot Hi-Vis pill if necessary,
                take survey and POOH.

             Use following parameters to drill this hole section:

             -WOB = 10-50 klb
               -RPM = 90-150
               -GPM= 900-1100

             Conduct Wire-line logging as program.
              If the hole dictates, Perform condition trip for running casing.
             Retrieve wear bushing.
             R/U casing running tools and equipment.
             P/U and M/U float shoe and float collar, Thread-lock all shoe track connections.
             Function test floating equipment.
             Run 13-3/8″ casing (68#, N-80, BTC) and install centralizers as per tally.
              Fill up casing every 5-10 joints. Monitor hook loads during running of the casing to
                 detect excessive drag.
             Break circulation when run to mid depth of borehole.

             Rig up cementing head, connect cement line and pressure test same (Test Pressure:

              If no slurry returns to surface, top-fill job is required.

             N/U 20-3/4″ 3k x 13-5/8″ 5K C.H.S. and P/T p-seals (75% of casing collapse
                pressure rate).
             P/T BOP system and C.H.S.
             Retrieve test plug and install wear bushing.

            Note:
            -Perform BOP's test on each 14 days as per NIOC Policy.
            -Adjust mud weight with respect to the hole condition.
         -TD should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole for probable fillings.
           -The Section TD maybe changed based on formation tops detected by the well site
               geologist.
          -Do not forget to strap pipe while POOH.
          -POOH. If observed any indication of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and ream to make
             sure that the hole condition is good.

3  12-1/4” Hole Section / 9-5/8” Production Casing

      3.1 General

     Casing Point               9-5/8” Casing@ 1497m MD(47#, L-80(1), Gas Tight)
     Formation               Gachsaran
                                Tight Hole, Hole stability problem, Salt water influx, Pipe
     Risks & Hole problems      sticking, High pressure salt water contamination, Calcium
                                 contamination.
     Deviation control          Single shot each 100 m & Multi shot each 300 m.
     Logging                 GR/DT/Deviation Tool , CBL/VDL/CCL/GR
                                    Salt Saturated mud
                MW    90 -110 pcf
      Drilling Fluid
                                    Vis.    45-55 Sec/Q.
                       PH     9.5-10
                   WOB    10- 40 Klb
                     RPM    90-150
      Drilling Parameters
                    GPM    600-800
                      PDC bit drilling parameters will be issued in due time.

      3.2 Formation Sampling

           From 1202 m to section TD: on each 2-5 meters one sample would be taken.
             Note: As per well site Geologist request, the sample program may be changed.

      3.3 Sequence of Operations

             M/U & RIH 12-1/4″ BHA to tag TOC.
              Circulate. Drill plugs, float collar and cement to 7m above float shoe with reduced
                parameters.
             Displace new mud and circulate to condition same.
              If observe NO bumping while 13 3/8” cement job, Circulate and uniform mud then
               performed P/T 13 3/8” casing integrity test to 1500 PSI.
              Drill out cement shoe track, shoe and cement of rat hole, Watch carefully for any
                 indication of abnormal torque, since there might be a possibility of shoe being
              backed off. In case, report immediately.
             Continue drill 15-20m new formation.
              Circulate hole clean It. POOH to casing shoe.
             Perform FIT as per procedure (EMW= Max hole MW+2 PCF).

             POOH.

4  8 1/2” Hole Section / 7” Production Liner

      4.1 General

 Liner point             7” Liner @ 2804m, MD / 2679 m TVD« 2m TVD above Top of Sarvak 3 »
 Liner specification:        7”, 29#, L-80(1)- (6-1/8” Special drift)
 Formation               Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak 1, 2.
                          Loss of circulation, Tight hole, Pipe sticking, H2S and poisonous gas.
 Risks & Hole problems     Pipe twisting off or corrosion by sour gas, Shale and hole instability
                           problem, Shale swelling, Pack off, Caving Hole.
 Deviation control     MWD
 Logging                GR/DT/Deviation Tool , CBL/VDL/CCL/GR
 Coring                         ------
                                           Glycol/KCL/Polymer MUD
               MW       77-79 pcf
 Drilling Fluid                  Vis.       40-50 Sec/Q
                     PV        13-18cp
                      YP        12-18 Lb/100ft²
                 WOB       10-30 Klb
                   RPM       90-150
 Drilling Parameters       GPM      400-600
                            Directional drilling parameters will be specified by directional driller.
                  PDC bit drilling parameters will be issued in due time.

      4.2 Formation Sampling

          On each 2-5 meters one sample would be taken. In addition to this general rule,
       geologist may decide to take more samples.

      4.3 Sequence of Operations

             M/U 8-1/2″ drilling BHA, RIH to tag TOC.
              Drill out cement to 7m above float shoe with reduced parameters.
              If observe NO bumping while 9 5/8” cement job, Circulate and uniform mud then
               performed P/T 9 5/8” casing integrity test to 3000 PSI.
             Continue drill out cement and shoe.
             Displace and adjust new mud in hole @ 3 steps and after each step observe well.
             Commence drilling formation with reduced parameters.

             Continue drill 15-20m new formation.

             Wiper trip will be performed once every 300m footages drilled or depending on hole

             M/U and RIH 8-1/2" directional BHA and function test same on surface. RIH to

             Tag bottom and P/U to confirm the liner setting depth.
             Hold PJSM for cementing.
             R/U cementing head and connect cementing line (Testing pressure:5000 psi)
             Slowly break circulation and circulate hole clean with optimal flow rate.
             Set liner hanger and release running tool following the instruction of L.H engineer.
             Cement liner as per program.

2  26" Hole Section

 2.1 Section Discussion

   The 26” hole is planned to drill with Bentonite Hi Vis. Mud to Section TD at 150 m to run 20”

    surface casing.

    After drilling of each stand, 40 bbl. of High viscous pill should be pumped to clean hole or more

      if the hole condition dictates. Once TD is reached, pump 80 bbl. of high viscous pill, circulate it

    out and prior to pull out the BHA for running the 20" casing,

    General practices to help avoiding hole to pack off during connection:

           *Circulate out the whole with 100 bbl. Hi-Vis pill prior to any Totco run in hole

           *Prior to POOH for BHA change, spot sufficient amount of Hi-Vis pill at bottom

 2.2  Drilling Mud

                              Salt water Hi-Vis pill sweeps and displacement mud

        Chemical                                    Function

             Drill water                         should have less than 600 ppm calcium

        Caustic soda                                          Alkalinity control

        Soda Ash                                Hardness control

          Bentonite                                             Viscosifier

 2.3 Mixing procedures

  1.  Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

  2.  Treat out any hardness greater than 600 ppm with soda ash (1-3 lb. /bbl.)

  3. Add 25-35lb/bbl. of bentonite and allow hydrating for as long as possible

  4.  Raise PH to ±11 with additional of caustic soda (1-2 lb. /bbl.)

3  17 1/2" Hole Section

 3.1 Section Discussion

   The 17 1/2” hole section will be drilled from 20" casing shoe at 150 ± m to an estimated depth of

    1202 ± m. The interval consists of two formations, Aghajari, and Gachsaran formations. The

    formations are predominately made up of Sandstone, Clay stone, Marl, and anhydrate.

    This section will be drilled down using bentonite mud to 300 m from previous section. After

     drilling 300 m interval, mud should be dumped and the hole will be displaced with KCl /

    Polymer system and drilling continues to the depth of 1202 m (MD)(55 MT in Gachsaran

    formation) .

    Before entering Gachsaran formation density should reach to 75 PCF and fluid loss control is the

     critical issue while drilling this section and should be maintained as program.

 3.2  Drilling Hazards

    Wellbore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

        * Swelling clays

          *Partial losses

   3.2.1  Hole instability

    In addition to KCl mud and PHPA can be added to provide extra inhibition in clay section.

   PHPA acts as encapsulating polymer, minimizing the hydration dispersion of cutting. Pump low

    Vis and hivis pill periodic

   3.2.2  Lost circulation

   The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. In the event of seepage losses, treat

    system with a fine to medium grade of LCM at 10 lb. /bbl. to 20 lb. /bbl. In the event of moderate

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 75

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

     to high rate of wellbore loss, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and spot over thief

    zone. Concentration of LCM may need to recalculate.

         Biocide                                          Anti-bacterial

       PHPA                                              Inhibitor

 3.4 Mixing procedures

   3.4.1   Interval of 150-300 m

        1. Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

        2. Treat out any hardness greater than 600 ppm with soda ash (1-3b/bbl.)

        3. Add 25-30lb/bbl. of bentonite and allow hydrating for as long as possible

        4. Raise PH to ±11 with additional of caustic soda (1-2 lb. /bbl.)

   3.4.2   Interval of 300-1202 m

    After drilling up to 300 m, displace the previous mud with the new mud:

  1.  Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

  2.  Treat out any hardness greater than 800 ppm with soda ash 1-3 ppb

  3. Add KCl for shale inhibitor to reach %3-%5 w/w about10.5-18 ppb.(The calculated amount of

    KCl as per the lower concentration mentioned in the program)

  4. Add NaCl to M.W 74 PCF

  5. Add Potato starch 9 -11 ppb then wait 10 to 15 minute on mixing

  6. Use 1-2 ppb PAC-L and wait 5 minute

  7. Use 1-2 ppb PAC-R and wait 5 minute

  8. Use Barite to weight up above 74 PCF as needed

  9.  Add about 0.5-2 ppb caustic soda to control pH and reduce hardness, PH value should not

     exceed more than 10.

 10. Add anti-bacterial "the useful value will say after determine situation"

Notices

                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

  1. Make PHPA pill in reserve tank w/0.5 - 1 ppb solution and add to system while drilling

  2.  If use of potato starch cannot control F.L replace it with Modified starch.

  3. Use solid control equipment while drilling (Especially the centrifuges).

  4. Add materials one by one as shown same as above.

  5. Pump Low Vis and Hivis pill after each stand.

4  12 1/4" Hole Section

 4.1 Section Discussion

   The 12 1/4" hole section will be drilled from 13 3/8" casing shoe at 1202± m to an estimated

    section TD of 1497± m (MD) (base of Gachsaran formation in the anhydrite below the salt bed)

   The 9 5/8" casing shall cover Gachsaran formation. Mud from previous section should be

   dumped after drill out cement; the hole will be displaced with Salt saturate mud system and then

     drill this section.

   The section will be drilled through Gachsaran formation. The formation composed of Anhydrite,

    Claystone, Marl, and salt. The risk of influx in this section is very high and mud should be

    adjusted to control the flow. Barite stock should be kept at maximum in this phase. Intensive care

    should be paid to the mud in order to have proper hole cleaning.

 4.2  Drilling Hazards

     Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

           * Hole instability

           * Salt water influx

   4.2.1  Hole Instability

      It is important to maintain mud weight as per pore pressure curve dictates in the drilling program.

    Ensure the concentrations are as per program and that these are determined and reported every

     tour. This should provide optimum hole instability and less hole problem.

   4.2.2  Salt Water Influx

    There is possibility of salt water flow in Gachsaran formation. This has caused severe

    contamination to the mud which necessitated a lot of dump and dilution and high products

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 81

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

    consumption. Therefore, sufficient stock of products (especially barite) is to be on board to

    control flow.

 4.3  Drilling mud

                                               Salt saturated mud

         Chemical                                    Function

   Water                                    should have less than 1000 ppm calcium

   Salt                                                 Weighting agent

   Soda ash                                         Hardness control

   Starch(green)                                              Fluid loss control

   Lime                                    PH control

   Barite                                               Weighting agent

   Xanthan Gum (xc pol.)                                 Rheological control

 4.4 Mixing procedure

   1.Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

  2.Treat out any hardness greater than 1000 ppm with soda ash

  3.Weight up with salt up to 74 PCF adjusted with the whole condition

  4.Add Potato starch 10 - 12 ppb then wait 10 to 15 minute on mixing.

  5.Add Barite to weight up as program

  6.Raise PH to 9 -9.5 with additional of Lime

  7.Add XC-polymer to increase YP and viscosity as needed.

Notices:

5  8 1/2" Hole Section

 5.1 Section Discussion

   The 8 1/2" hole section will be drilled from 9 5/8" casing shoe at 1497 ± m to an estimated

    section TD of 2804± m (MD. The 7" liner shall cover Asmari, pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, and

    Sarvak 1 to 2 Mud from previous section should be dumped after drilling out cement; the hole

     will be displaced with Glycol / KCl / Polymer system.

 5.2  Drilling Hazards

     Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

         * Possible losses

         * Present of H2S

   5.2.1  Lost circulation

   The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Partial to severe losses are expected in

    Sarvak formation and therefore sufficient stocks of acid soluble graded LCM, such as fine

   CaCO3 and oyster shell should be kept on board. In case of seepage losses, the system should be

     treated with medium grade of LCM. For moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at different

    graded concentrations and spot across the thief zone.

    In order to avoid LCM plugging MWD tools a circulation sub should be installed in the drill

     string. The MWD engineer is to advise on the size and concentration of LCM that can be pumped

    through MWD tools. Ensure that the drilling displace all LCM pill out of string prior to shutting

   down the mud pump.

   5.2.2  Presence of H2S

    Treat system with H2S scavenger, keep high alkalinity and use Garret gas train to determine

    concentration.

 5.3  Drilling Mud

                                     Glycol - KCl polymer mud system

         Chemical                                    Function

             Water                           should have less than 600 ppm calcium

                 Salt                                      Weighting agent

           Soda ash                                 Hardness control

        Potassium chloride                                            Inhibitor

           Starch(HT)                                        Fluid loss control

           Limestone                                   Weighting agent

         PAC LV                                         Fluid loss control

      Xanthan Gum (xc pol.)                              Rheological control

           Caustic soda                          PH control

               Bit lube                                           Lubricant

             Glycol                                                   Inhibitor

 5.4 Mixing procedure

     1. Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

     2. Treat out any hardness greater than 600 ppm with soda ash

     3. Add KCl for shale inhibitor to reach %7 - 8% w/w (about 25.6 – 29 ppb)

     4. Add NaCl to weight up fluid to 73 PCF

     5. Add 10 - 12 ppb starch HT and let to be mixed for 10 to 15 minute

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 86

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

     6. use 1 - 3 ppb PAC-L and mix it for at least 5 minute

     7. use Limestone to weight up above 73 PCF as needed

     8. add 1 - 3 ppb Xc-polymer as needed

     9. Shall use %3 - %5 v/v Glycol to active system. (Before using coordinate with office)

    10. Add about 0.5 - 2 ppb caustic soda to control pH and reduce hardness, PH value should not

    exceed more than 10.

       1- Use solid control equipment while drilling (Especially the centrifuges).

       2-   If need add Bit Lubricant solution (%2 - %3 v/v), (Before use coordinate with office).

6  6 1/8" Hole Section

 6.1 Section Discussion

   The 6 ⅛" hole section will be drilled from 7" liner shoe at 2804 ± m to an estimated section TD

    of 3600± m (MD). The fluid from previous section will be carried and utilization of a Glycol /

   KCl / Polymer mud system should firmly inhibit shale and provides excellent properties drilling

    and inhibition properties.

 6.2  Drilling Hazards

     Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

          * Possible losses

          * Present of H2S

   6.2.1  Lost circulation

   The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Partial to severe losses are expected in

    Sarvak formation and therefore sufficient stocks of acid soluble graded LCM, such as fine

   CaCO3 oyster shell should be kept on board. In case of seepage losses, the system should be

     treated with fine and medium grade of LCM. For moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at

     different graded concentrations and spot across the thief zone.

    In order to avoid LCM plugging MWD tools a circulation sub should be installed in the drill

     string. The MWD engineer is to advise on the size and concentration of LCM that can be pumped

    through MWD tools. Ensure that the drilling displace all LCM pill out of string prior to shutting

   down the mud pump.

   6.2.2  Presence of H2S

    Treat system with H2S scavenger, keep high alkalinity and use Garret gas train to determine

    concentration.

7  Solid control
1) Each kind of solid control equipment should be designed to process 100% to 150% of the
   maximum anticipated circulating volume of mud. Shale shaker, de-sander, de-silter and cleaner
    are required to run all the time during drilling and centrifuge is recommended to run at least 80%
    of drilling time.

2) The sand content and MBT should be kept as low as possible by solid removal and dilution. Batch
   wise dilution (replacement of 20-30% of the total drilling fluid volume in one circulation) is more
    effective than continuous dilution.

3) The detailed requirement of solid control equipment are as follows:

                             The capacity of Solids Control Equipment

       Shale Shakers(3)         ≥60 L/S(Single screen)            60-200mesh

       Degasser(1)                   ≥200m3/h

       Desander(1)                   ≥160m3/h            Cut Range 44-74Microns

       Desilter(1)                    ≥160m3/h            Cut Range 15-44Microns

      M-speed centrifuge(1)            ≥6m3/h              Cut Point 5-7 Microns

      H-speed centrifuge(1)            ≥4m3/h              Cut Point 2-5 Microns

8  Guidelines for Kill mud preparation
1) The kill fluid should be prepared prior to commence of each hole section, If well flow observed.

The density of kill fluid is determined usually heavier than the formation coefficient should be

recalculated this coefficient by new situation. Volume of kill fluid is following the regulation of

South Azadegan Oil Field:

For pore pressure coefficient of formation increase and pay zone without H2S, the volume of kill mud

will changed and need to recalculate.

If in case pore pressure coefficient of formation increase and pay zone has H2S, the volume of kill

mud will changed and need to recalculate.

2) The following emergency or contingency materials should be kept on the rig site at all times to

supplement the proposed mud system. Kick, lost circulation, stuck pipe scenarios, lubrication and

general purpose operations may require the following products to be on storage or standby.

                                          Kill Fluid and Chemical Storage

9  Formation or Pore Pressure and Mud Weight
Primary Well Control relies on the hydrostatic pressure of drilling fluid to balance the formation pore

pressure. In order to counteract any minor pressure reductions on bottom due to swabbing effect when

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                               Page 94

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

tripping, it is customary to maintain a hydrostatic column with a pressure slightly exceeding the pore

pressure of the exposed formation. This overbalance, safety margin, or trip margin, Ptm, varies

depending on circumstances but it is generally of the order of 200 psi for conventional operations.

The required density Pf to control a formation at a depth, D, with a pressure, Po, is given by: Pf =

(Po+Ptm), or Df = (Po+Ptm)/D

To gas cut or contaminated mud, Df = Do + (0.07-0.15) sg,

To oil and/or salt water contaminated mud, Df = Do + (0.05-0.10) sg;

                      Bit Size:      12-1/4”
                      Bit Code:     1-1-5, S223 (PDC,Steel body, 5 blades,19 mm cutter)
                             M323 (PDC,Matrix body, 6 blades,19 mm, 16 mm cutter)
                     Nozzles:    TFA= 0.7 sq. in For pdc bit

            The following drilling parameters are recommended to optimize drilling:

            WOB = 10 – 40 Klbs
             RPM = 90 - 150RPM for rock bit & 180 RPM for PDC bit
            GPM = 600-800

4  Casing running procedure

      4.1 26” Hole Section / 20” Surface Casing
String Design
 20” Casing Stab-in Float Shoe, 94 ppf, K-55, BTC.

 ±150m (Approx.13 Jts) 20” Casing, 94 ppf, K-55, BTC. (Est. length for each joint of CSG=12m) to

   Surface.

 26” x 20” bow Centralisers on each 2 Joints.

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the Stab-in Float Shoe for proper operation.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment are available and in good operation condition.

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Ensure that all casing joints have been drifted.

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of Operations
 Conduct safety meeting with all crews involved.

 Rig up casing handling and running equipment.

 P/U 20”, 94 ppf, K-55, BTC, float shoe, soft make up on first joint, P/U and make up to the optimum

   torque. Do not engage the tong close to bottom of shoe. Use thread lock compound for shoe.

 Run 20” casing & centralisers according to tally.

 Run casing at speed calculated by WSS. A rate of 5 Joints/hr could be used as a guide.

 Work slightly through any tight spots if necessary. Circulate if deemed necessary.

 Fill casing while running in each joint.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                              Page 110

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

      4.2 17-1/2" Hole Section / 13 3/8” Intermediate Casing
String Design
 13-3/8” Casing float Shoe, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC.

 Two Joints (Approx. ±24m) of 13-3/8” Casing, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC.

 13-3/8” Casing float Collar, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC.

 ±1178m (Approx. ±98Jts) of 13-3/8” Casing, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC. (Est. length for each joint of CSG=12m).

 Bow Centralizer on the first 4 joints.

 Bow Centralisers 1 ea every four joints.

 Rigid centraliser on third and fourth joints from top.

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the float shoe and collar for proper operation.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment are available and in good condition.

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Check the plugs fins not to be squeezed or damaged.

 Ensure that all casing joints have been drifted. Including 13 3/8”swedge (If required to wash casing down).

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure. Ensure that the air supply for cement is functioning properly.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of operations

 Rig up casing handling and running equipment.

 M/U 13 3/8”, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC, float shoe. If it is not already made up on joint, soft make up on first

    joint, P/U and make up to the optimum make up torque based on triangle using casing tong. Do not engage

   the tong close to bottom of shoe. Repeat the same for collar.

 Use thread locking compound for shoe track plus two joints on top of float collar. Take special care on

   thread locking these joints, since the consequence of improper locking would be disastrous.

 Run two joints of 13 3/8”, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC casings.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                              Page 111

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

 Make up 13 3/8”, 68 ppf, BTC, float collar.

 Run Approx. ±1178m (±98 Jts) of 13 3/8” Casing, 68ppf, N-80, BTC. (Est. length for each joint of

  CSG=12m).

Note: Circulate through casing after running of totally 5 Jts for one Casing capacity.

 Note: Manage to have one meters of rat hole and 1.5 meters of casing stick up on rig floor.

 Pump one and half the casing volume @ 20” shoe and on TD.

 Run casing at speed calculated by Company Man. a rate of 10 Joints/hr could be used as a guide.

 Using stabbing board & single joint elevator to run casing would be time saving.

 Work slightly through any tight spots. Circulate if deemed necessary.

 Precautionary wash the last joint.

 When landed the casing, M/U cement head. Load the plugs accordingly. Check the flag to operate properly.

      4.3 12-1/4" Hole Section / 9 5/8” Production Casing
String Design
    9 5/8” Casing float Shoe, 47 ppf, L-80(1), TPCQ.

   Two Joints (Approx.±24m) of 9 5/8” Casing, 47 ppf, L-80(1), TPCQ.

    9 5/8” float Collar, 47 ppf, L-80(1), TPCQ.

   ±1473m (Approx. ±123 Joints) of 9 5/8” Casing, , 47 ppf, L-80(1), TPCQ (Est. length for each joint of

     CSG=12m).

    Bow Centralizer on the first 4 joints.

    Bow Centralisers 1 ea every four joints

     Rigid centralisers on third and fourth joints from top.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS-084(S372)                                              Page 112

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#084 (S372 – Area I ) Rev 0.0                                                             PEDEC

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the shoe & collar for proper operation.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment is available and in good condition.

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Check the plugs fins not to be squeezed or damaged.

 Ensure that all casing joints have been drifted, including 9 5/8”swedge (If required to wash casing down).

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure. Ensure that the air supply system for cement is functioning properly.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of Operations
 Rig up casing handling and running equipment, (Including jam system).

 P/U 9 5/8 float Shoe, 47 ppf, L-80(1),TPCQ, M/U to the optimum make up torque. Do not engage the tong

     close to bottom of shoe.

 Use thread locking compound for shoe track plus two joints on top of collar.

 Run two joints of 9 5/8”, 47 ppf, L-80(1),TPCQ,casing.

 Make up 9 5/8”, 47 ppf, L-80(1), TPCQ, float collar.

 Run Approx. ±1473m (±123 Joints) of 9 5/8”, 47 ppf, L-80(1), TPCQ casing.

   Note: Circulate through casing after running of totally 5 Jts for one Casing capacity.

 Manage to have 1m of rat hole and 1.5 meters of casing stick up on rig floor.

 Pump a complete casing volume at shoe and on the section TD.

 Run casing at speed calculated by Company Man. A rate of 10 Jts/hr could be used as a guide.

 Work slightly through any tight spots if necessary. Circulate if deemed necessary.

 Precautionary wash the last joint.

 When landed the casing, M/U cement head. Load the plugs accordingly. Check the flag to operate properly.
